# SEST combat cost list

Research reviewed 23-24 October 2026. Generated by `integration/combat-costs/build_pack.py` from the cost builder (`integration/common/costs.py`): the Combat Cost Tracker's captured catalogs, SEST's reviewed mappings and researched estimates (`integration/combat-costs/mappings.json`), the research record (`integration/combat-costs/research.json`) and SEST_Campaign's built missions and rosters. Do not edit it by hand: `tools/build_all.py` rebuilds it after the campaign. `cost-list.csv` beside it holds the same rows.

- **Some sources could not be read.** This environment's network policy blocked them (proxy 403 and DNS failures). No price that rests on such a page is shipped; the figures are listed under Gaps as candidates.
- **Researched estimates are SEST's**, not the tracker author's. Each is labelled with its method, year and sources, below and in the comment above its section in the shipped catalog.
- **Historical** is the tracker's rounded service-entry-era USD (`WeaponCosts`, `VesselCosts`, `AircraftCosts`). **Current** is the explicit `CurrentYearCost` in 2026 USD; it never falls back to the historical price. Ships are priced fitted, without ammunition or embarked aircraft. Unknown is never free, and purchase points are never converted to USD.
- **Price from**: `tracker catalog` (the author's entry), `inline in unit file`, `SEST mapping from <id>` (an author price copied exactly from that entry), `SEST researched estimate`, or `unknown` with the reason.
- Aircraft have no tracker category; the tracker splits planes and helicopters itself.

## Summary

| list | items | both prices | historical only | current only | neither |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Weapons SEST ships | 138 | 129 | 0 | 0 | 9 |
| Vessels SEST ships | 339 | 320 | 0 | 0 | 19 |
| Aircraft SEST ships | 37 | 35 | 0 | 0 | 2 |
| Vessels SEST's missions field | 163 | 148 | 0 | 0 | 15 |
| Aircraft SEST's missions field | 139 | 119 | 0 | 2 | 18 |
| Land units SEST ships | 27 | - | - | - | not priced: the tracker does not price land platforms |
| Weapons carried by fielded units with a missing price | 256 | - | 0 | 12 | 244 |

Shipped by SEST: 59 researched estimates, 133 reviewed mappings and 22 spelling matches that copy an author price.

## SEST researched estimates

59 shipped. Methods: published price, comparable recent purchase, documented replacement estimate, CPI conversion, model estimate. A CPI conversion uses the CPI-U series of the named source; a model estimate is a comparison, not a published price.

- **civ_a320** (aircraft): historical unknown; current 55,000,000 (documented replacement estimate, source year 2026). Airbus A320ceo airliner: no 1988 price could be read, so the historical price stays unknown; current is the successor A320neo's new-aircraft market value of USD 50-60M (Cirium/IBA via Boersen-Zeitung, 16 Oct 2026), midpoint. List prices are not used: discounts of 20-50% are usual. Sources: Flugzeugfinanzierer ACG bestellt bei Airbus 75 A320neo (Borsen-Zeitung, 16 Oct 2026): https://www.boersen-zeitung.de/unternehmen-branchen/acg-will-75-maschinen-aus-der-airbus-modellfamilie-a320neo-kaufen; Borsen-Zeitung (16 Oct 2026), list vs discount: https://www.boersen-zeitung.de/unternehmen-branchen/acg-will-75-maschinen-aus-der-airbus-modellfamilie-a320neo-kaufen; Airbus publishes 2018 average aircraft list prices (CAPA, 16 Jan 2018), context only: https://centreforaviation.com/news/airbus-publishes-2018-average-aircraft-list-prices-757058.
- **civ_a330** (aircraft): historical unknown; current 107,000,000 (documented replacement estimate, source year 2024). Airbus A330-300 airliner: no 1994 price could be read, so the historical price stays unknown; current is the successor A330-900neo's full-life market value of about USD 107M (Cirium Ascend, June 2024). List prices are not used. Sources: Is A330neo Coming to China? - A Suitability Analysis (Cirium, 17 Jun 2024): https://www.cirium.com/thoughtcloud/ascend-consultancy-weekly-team-perspective-is-a330neo-coming-to-china-suitability-analysis/; Airbus publishes 2018 average aircraft list prices (CAPA, 16 Jan 2018), context only: https://centreforaviation.com/news/airbus-publishes-2018-average-aircraft-list-prices-757058.
- **jp_f-2a_late** (aircraft): historical 80,000,000 (published price, reference year 1996); current 171,000,000 (CPI conversion, source year 1996). Mitsubishi F-2A: GAO's figure for the approved production programme, about USD 80M per aircraft in constant 1996 dollars at 110 yen/$ (GAO/NSIAD-97-76); a conservative lower anchor. Current: 80M x 334.4 / 156.9 (CPI-U 1996) = 170.50M, rounded. Sources: GAO/NSIAD-97-76, U.S.-Japan Fighter Aircraft: Agreement on F-2 Production (govinfo): https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/GAOREPORTS-NSIAD-97-76/html/GAOREPORTS-NSIAD-97-76.htm; GAO/NSIAD-97-76 footnote 6: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/GAOREPORTS-NSIAD-97-76/html/GAOREPORTS-NSIAD-97-76.htm; F-2 (航空機), Japanese Wikipedia (cross-check only): https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-2_(航空機); Minneapolis Fed, Consumer Price Index 1913-: https://www.minneapolisfed.org/about-us/monetary-policy/inflation-calculator/consumer-price-index-1913-.
- **mm_av-8b_plus** (aircraft): historical 22,400,000 (published price, reference year 1991); current 55,000,000 (CPI conversion, source year 1991). Italian AV-8B Harrier II Plus: same radar Harrier II Plus as spa_av-8b_plus; FY1991 average unit flyaway cost USD 22.4M (GAO/NSIAD-96-49), as in SEST's reviewed Spanish estimate; not an Italian contract price. Current: 22.4M x 334.4 / 136.2 (CPI-U 1991) = 54.997M, rounded. Sources: SEST researched estimate spa_av-8b_plus (shipped): file:integration/combat-costs/mappings.json; GAO/NSIAD-96-49, Navy Aviation: AV-8B Harrier Remanufacture Strategy Is Not the Most Cost-Effective Option (govinfo): https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/GAOREPORTS-NSIAD-96-49/html/GAOREPORTS-NSIAD-96-49.htm; mm_av-8b_plus English description (mod 3505420313): file:mods-source/3505420313/language_en/aircraft_names.ini.
- **mv22b_osprey** (aircraft): historical 62,300,000 (published price, reference year 2008); current 72,300,000 (comparable recent purchase, source year 2018). MV-22B (IOC June 2007): FY2008-FY2012 multiyear contract, USD 10.4B for 167 V-22s (CRS RL31384); current is the 2018 multiyear contract, prime airframe only (engines government-furnished), so a lower bound. Historical: 10.4B / 167 = 62.28M. Current: 4,191,533,822 / 58 = 72.27M. Sources: CRS RL31384, V-22 Osprey Tilt-Rotor Aircraft Program (Apr 2012), EveryCRSReport: https://www.everycrsreport.com/reports/RL31384.html; CRS RL31384 (Apr 2012), multiyear quantity: https://www.everycrsreport.com/reports/RL31384.html; CRS RL31384 (Apr 2012), IOC and SAR cross-check: https://www.everycrsreport.com/reports/RL31384.html; CRS RL31384 quoting GAO-09-482 (cross-check only): https://www.everycrsreport.com/reports/RL31384.html; Bell Boeing to begin U.S. Navy CMV-22B production work under $4 billion contract (Airframer, 2018): https://www.airframer.com/news/release/41903.
- **rok_f-50_ph** (aircraft): historical 35,200,000 (published price, reference year 2014); current 57,100,000 (comparable recent purchase, source year 2025). KAI FA-50PH (Philippine Air Force): 2014 contract of USD 422M for 12 (defensa.com); current is the June 2025 follow-on for 12 more, which includes logistics support, so an upper bound. Historical: 422M / 12 = 35.17M. Current: KRW 975.3B / 1422.44083333 (tracker KRW Rate2025) / 12 = 57.14M. Sources: Corea del Sur cierra un contrato de 422 millones de dolares con Filipinas para la venta de 12 aviones FA-50 (defensa.com, 24 Feb 2014): https://www.defensa.com/africa-asia-pacifico/corea-sur-cierra-contrato-422-millones-dolares-filipinas-para-12; Philippines Signs $700 Million Deal for 12 More FA-50PH Jets from South Korea (Overt Defense, 11 Jun 2025): https://www.overtdefense.com/2025/06/11/philippines-signs-700-million-deal-for-12-more-fa-50ph-jets-from-south-korea; Overt Defense (11 Jun 2025), 2014 order: https://www.overtdefense.com/2025/06/11/philippines-signs-700-million-deal-for-12-more-fa-50ph-jets-from-south-korea.
- **spa_av-8b_plus** (aircraft): historical 22,400,000 (published price, reference year 1991); current 55,000,000 (CPI conversion, source year 1991). Spanish EAV-8B Harrier II Plus: same radar-model Harrier II Plus as the USMC's, FY1991 average unit flyaway cost USD 22.4M (GAO/NSIAD-96-49); not a Spanish contract price. Current: 22.4M x 334.4 / 136.2 (CPI-U 1991) = 54.997M, rounded. Sources: GAO/NSIAD-96-49, Navy Aviation: AV-8B Harrier Remanufacture Strategy Is Not the Most Cost-Effective Option (govinfo): https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/GAOREPORTS-NSIAD-96-49/html/GAOREPORTS-NSIAD-96-49.htm; GAO/NSIAD-96-49, variant introduction: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/GAOREPORTS-NSIAD-96-49/html/GAOREPORTS-NSIAD-96-49.htm; McDonnell Douglas AV-8B Harrier II (Wikipedia), Spanish Navy section: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McDonnell_Douglas_AV-8B_Harrier_II; Minneapolis Fed, Consumer Price Index 1913-: https://www.minneapolisfed.org/about-us/monetary-policy/inflation-calculator/consumer-price-index-1913-.
- **jmsdf_aoe_mashuu** (vessel, AOE): historical 399,000,000 (published price, reference year 2000); current 481,000,000 (comparable recent purchase, source year 2025). Mashu-class AOE (FY2000 plan): published build cost JPY 43.0bn (ja.wikipedia) at the tracker's JPY Rate2000; current is ATLA's 2025 contract for the larger 14,500 t successor AOE, an upper-side proxy. Historical: 43e9 / 107.765498333 = 399.0M. Current: 72e9 / 149.657926891 (tracker JPY Rate2025) = 481.1M. Sources: ましゅう型補給艦 (Japanese Wikipedia): https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%81%BE%E3%81%97%E3%82%85%E3%81%86%E5%9E%8B%E8%A3%9C%E7%B5%A6%E8%89%A6; ましゅう (補給艦) (Japanese Wikipedia): https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%81%BE%E3%81%97%E3%82%85%E3%81%86_(%E8%A3%9C%E7%B5%A6%E8%89%A6); Japan's MHI wins contract to build new replenishment ship and two Upgraded FFM (Naval News, 16 May 2025): https://www.navalnews.com/?p=72592; 海自が巨大な「新型補給艦」導入へ (trafficnews.jp) - budget alternative, not used: https://trafficnews.jp/post/546291.
- **jmsdf_ddh_hyuga** (vessel, DDH): historical 920,000,000 (published price, reference year 2004); current 1,629,000,000 (CPI conversion, source year 2004). Hyuga-class DDH: published USD 920m in 2004 dollars (USNI Proceedings, Oct 2007); 2004 is the budget year, the class entered service in 2009. Current: 920M x 334.4 / 188.9 (CPI-U 2004) = 1,628.6M, rounded. Sources: Combat Fleets, Proceedings October 2007 (Eric Wertheim, USNI): https://www.usni.org/magazines/proceedings/2007/october/combat-fleets; Consumer Price Index, 1913- (Federal Reserve Bank of Minneapolis): https://www.minneapolisfed.org/about-us/monetary-policy/inflation-calculator/consumer-price-index-1913-.
- **plan_lpd_type_071** (vessel, LPD): historical 162,000,000 (CPI conversion, reference year 2007); current 262,000,000 (CPI conversion, source year 2019). Type 071 LPD (first service 2007): export-family anchor, the 2019 Type 071E for Thailand at Bt6.1bn (about USD 200m, Naval News), carried back to 2007 and forward to 2026 by CPI; low confidence. Historical: 200M x 207.3 / 255.7 (CPI-U 2007 / 2019) = 162.1M. Current: 200M x 334.4 / 255.7 = 261.6M, rounded. Sources: Royal Thai Navy Procures Type 071E LPD from China's CSSC (Naval News, 16 Sep 2019): https://www.navalnews.com/naval-news/2019/09/royal-thai-navy-procures-type-071e-lpd-from-chinas-cssc/; Consumer Price Index, 1913- (Federal Reserve Bank of Minneapolis): https://www.minneapolisfed.org/about-us/monetary-policy/inflation-calculator/consumer-price-index-1913-.
- **pn_ffg-06** (vessel, FFG): historical 250,000,000 (published price, reference year 2021); current 297,000,000 (comparable recent purchase, source year 2025). Miguel Malvar class: published contract PHP 12.5bn (about USD 250m) per ship-platform, excluding munitions (2021); current is the Dec 2025 HD HHI contract for two frigates built to the same specification. Current: KRW 844.7bn / 2 / 1422.44083333 (tracker KRW Rate2025) = 296.9M, rounded. Sources: Miguel Malvar-class frigate (English Wikipedia): https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguel_Malvar-class_frigate; HD Hyundai Heavy Industries wins 844.7 billion won order to build two Philippine frigates (Chosun Biz, 26 Dec 2025): https://biz.chosun.com/en/en-industry/2025/12/26/YQ64R6RXBJDX7AM4Y2BD6NR6AU/.
- **pn_ffg-150** (vessel, FFG): historical 168,000,000 (published price, reference year 2016); current 235,000,000 (CPI conversion, source year 2016). Jose Rizal class: published Notice of Award PHP 15.745bn for two (USD 336.9m), excluding munitions (Aug 2016); the larger Miguel Malvar follow-on is not used as a current comparison. Historical: 336.912M / 2 = 168.456M. Current: 168.456M x 334.4 / 240.0 (CPI-U 2016) = 234.7M, rounded. Sources: Jose Rizal-class frigate (English Wikipedia): https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jose_Rizal-class_frigate; Jose Rizal-class frigate (English Wikipedia), lot split: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jose_Rizal-class_frigate; HHI Launched the 'Jose Rizal' Frigate for the Philippines Navy (Naval News, 24 May 2019): https://www.navalnews.com/?p=4306; Consumer Price Index, 1913- (Federal Reserve Bank of Minneapolis): https://www.minneapolisfed.org/about-us/monetary-policy/inflation-calculator/consumer-price-index-1913-.
- **pn_ffg-150_2** (vessel, FFG): historical 168,000,000 (published price, reference year 2016); current 235,000,000 (CPI conversion, source year 2016). Jose Rizal class with a VLS what-if fit: same hull and values as pn_ffg-150; the VLS fit is not priced. Same figures as pn_ffg-150. Sources: Jose Rizal-class frigate (English Wikipedia): https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jose_Rizal-class_frigate.
- **rfn_aor_pashin** (vessel, AO): historical 93,000,000 (published price, reference year 2013); current 134,000,000 (CPI conversion, source year 2013). Project 23130 Akademik Pashin (in service 2020): RUB 2.978bn lead-ship contract (bmpd) at the 2013 average 31.8614 RUB/USD. Historical: 2,978M / 31.8614 = 93.47M. Current: 93.47M x 334.980 / 232.957 (CPI-U Aug 2026 / 2013) = 134.4M, rounded. Sources: bmpd (CAST) LiveJournal, 22 Jan 2020: https://bmpd.livejournal.com/3911104.html; ExchangeRates.org.uk, US Dollar to Russian Rouble History: 2013: https://www.exchangerates.org.uk/USD-RUB-spot-exchange-rates-history-2013.html; US Inflation Calculator, CPI-U data 1913-2026 (BLS series): https://www.usinflationcalculator.com/inflation/consumer-price-index-and-annual-percent-changes-from-1913-to-2008/.
- **rn_aor_tide** (vessel, AO): historical 179,000,000 (published price, reference year 2012); current 261,000,000 (CPI conversion, source year 2012). Tide-class MARS tanker (in service from 2017): GBP 452m four-ship DSME contract (Hansard, 22 Feb 2012) at the 2012 average 1.5849 USD/GBP; probably understates the fitted ship (UK customisation excluded). Historical: 452M x 1.5849 / 4 = 179.09M. Current: 179.09M x 334.980 / 229.594 (CPI-U Aug 2026 / 2012) = 261.3M, rounded. Sources: Hansard, Commons Written Statement 'Royal Fleet Auxiliary', 22 Feb 2012 (Peter Luff): https://hansard.parliament.uk/Commons/2012-02-22/debates/12022245000007/RoyalFleetAuxiliary; ExchangeRates.org.uk, British Pound to US Dollar History: 2012: https://www.exchangerates.org.uk/GBP-USD-spot-exchange-rates-history-2012.html; US Inflation Calculator, CPI-U data 1913-2026 (BLS series): https://www.usinflationcalculator.com/inflation/consumer-price-index-and-annual-percent-changes-from-1913-to-2008/.
- **tha_cvl_chakri_naruebet** (vessel, CVL): historical 285,000,000 (published price, reference year 1993); current 660,000,000 (CPI conversion, source year 1993). HTMS Chakri Naruebet: published ship cost US$285M (1993; Forecast International via Wikipedia); the 7,100M THB 1992 contract (about US$277.5M) is a cross-check. Current: 285,000,000 x 334.4 (Minneapolis Fed CPI-U, 2026 estimate) / 144.5 (1993) = 659,543,253, rounded. Sources: HTMS Chakri Naruebet - Wikipedia (raw wikitext, infobox citing Forecast International 'Principe de Asturias Class - Archived 3/99', March 1998): https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=HTMS_Chakri_Naruebet&action=raw; HTMS Chakri Naruebet - Wikipedia (body, citing Carpenter & Wiencek, Asian Security Handbook 2000, p. 302): https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=HTMS_Chakri_Naruebet&action=raw; เรือหลวงจักรีนฤเบศร - Thai Wikipedia (raw wikitext): https://th.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=เรือหลวงจักรีนฤเบศร&action=raw; Minneapolis Fed - Consumer Price Index, 1913-: https://www.minneapolisfed.org/about-us/monetary-policy/inflation-calculator/consumer-price-index-1913-.
- **tha_ffg_naresuan** (vessel, FFG): historical 78,000,000 (published price, reference year 1990); current 200,000,000 (CPI conversion, source year 1990). Naresuan class: published 2,000M THB per ship (Wikipedia, from GlobalSecurity) at the tracker's THB Rate1990. Chinese build price: the ships were delivered incomplete and the US gun, Harpoon, Mk 32 and LW-08/STIR fitted in Thailand are probably excluded, so this understates the fitted ship; low confidence. Sources: Naresuan-class frigate - Wikipedia: https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Naresuan-class_frigate&action=raw; เรือหลวงตากสิน - Thai Wikipedia (raw wikitext, citing GlobalSecurity 'NARESUAN (TYPE 25T) (FFG)'): https://th.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=เรือหลวงตากสิน&action=raw; Thai navy frigate damaged during exercise - Thai PBS World, 1 Aug 2023: https://www.thaipbsworld.com/around-thailand/thai-navy-frigate-damaged-during-exercise; Thajské fregaty - Made in China (valka.cz) - contract date: https://www.valka.cz/14742-Thajske-fregaty-Made-in-China; Thajské fregaty - Made in China (valka.cz) - delivered incomplete: https://www.valka.cz/14742-Thajske-fregaty-Made-in-China; Combat Cost Tracker currencies.ini (mods-source/3807187341/systems/combatcosts/currencies.ini): file:mods-source/3807187341/systems/combatcosts/currencies.ini; Minneapolis Fed - Consumer Price Index, 1913-: https://www.minneapolisfed.org/about-us/monetary-policy/inflation-calculator/consumer-price-index-1913-.
- **tha_fs_ratanakosin** (vessel, FS): historical 72,000,000 (published price, reference year 1983); current 240,000,000 (CPI conversion, source year 1983). Ratanakosin class: published US$143M Tacoma Boatbuilding contract for two ships including design (Maritime Reporter, Oct 1983), halved; the design share slightly overstates the hull. Historical: 143M / 2 = 71.5M. Current: 71,500,000 x 334.4 / 99.6 (CPI-U 1983) = 240,056,225, rounded. (13 Jul 2026): https://www.thepricer.org/how-much-does-a-military-sea-drone-cost/.

Kaiser (Wikipedia): https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USNS_Henry_J._Kaiser; CRS R43546, Navy John Lewis (TAO-205) Class Oiler Shipbuilding Program, updated August 1, 2025 (EveryCRSReport mirror): https://www.everycrsreport.com/files/2025-08-01_R43546_cf25c2dbfed48e0cdcc7cf4f202015c5e5035a08.html; CRS R43546 (same revision): https://www.everycrsreport.com/files/2025-08-01_R43546_cf25c2dbfed48e0cdcc7cf4f202015c5e5035a08.html; CRS R43546, updated January 12, 2026 (congress.gov): https://www.congress.gov/crs-product/R43546.

Kraken K5 (concept): same-maker family anchor, the Royal Navy's K3 Scout contract of GBP 12.3m (USD 16.5m) for 20 boats including training and experimentation support; no K5 price published. 16.5M / 20 = 825,000, rounded. Sources: Royal Navy news, Navy to buy 20 uncrewed boats as testbeds for future operations (11 Mar 2026): https://www.royalnavy.mod.uk/news/2026/march/11/20260311-kraken-usv-procurement-announcement; Tectonic Defense, Kraken Secures £12.3M USV Contract with the Royal Navy (12 Mar 2026): https://www.tectonicdefense.com/kraken-secures-12-3m-usv-contract-with-the-royal-navy/; Tectonic Defense (same article): https://www.tectonicdefense.com/kraken-secures-12-3m-usv-contract-with-the-royal-navy/.

Fictional air-drop Mk 48 Mod 8: model estimate = USN FY2025 MK-48 gross weapon system unit cost $4,217K (PB2025 P-40 LI 3117; FY2025 buys 79 MOD 7 G&C/afterbody sets); air-drop kit not priced. 333,147,000 / 79 = 4,217,051, rounded; the FY2025 buy is MOD 7 hardware, and the gross unit cost is a line average that includes installation and support; the air-drop kit is not priced. Sources: Exhibit P-40/P-5, PB 2025 Navy, LI 3117 MK-48 Torpedo (March 2024); S3-hosted copy of the justification-book pages, re-fetched and read 2026-10-23: https://gemini-custom-report.s3.amazonaws.com/2025/PROC_NAVY_3117.pdf; Same P-5 (read): the FY2025 quantity is MOD 7 hardware: https://gemini-custom-report.s3.amazonaws.com/2025/PROC_NAVY_3117.pdf; Identity: 3801363152 language_en/ammunition_names.ini: file:mods-source/3801363152/language_en/ammunition_names.ini.

GBU-12 Paveway II (in service 1977): the same weapon as SEST's usn_gbu-12d estimate; Gulf War Air Power Survey vol. 5 unit cost USD 9,000 in 1991 dollars, carried back to 1976 and forward to 2026 by CPI. Historical: Wikipedia citing Deagel, medium-low confidence. Current: the FY2025 budget's about USD 4.2M (PB2025 P-40 gross weapon system unit cost 4,217K). - **usn_mk48_mod4_adcap** (weapon, Torpedo): historical 3,500,000 (model estimate, reference year 1988); current 4,200,000 (comparable recent purchase, source year 2025). Model estimate: Mk 48 Mod 4 refitted with the FY85 ADCAP kit, priced as the Mk 48 ADCAP unit cost $3.5M (FAS, flagged '(?)'; ADCAP operational 1988); current is the FY2025 Mk 48 Mod 7 unit cost of about $4.2M. FAS flags the USD 3.5M ADCAP figure '(?)' and gives no year; 1988 is ADCAP's service entry; low confidence. Sources: FAS Military Analysis Network: MK-48 Torpedo: https://man.fas.org/dod-101/sys/ship/weaps/mk-48.htm; Mark 48 torpedo - Wikipedia (infobox; ref 3 FAS): https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_48_torpedo; TWZ: Navy Wants A Cheap Heavy Torpedo That Can Be Stockpiled Fast (10 Apr 2024): https://www.twz.com/sea/navy-wants-a-cheap-heavy-torpedo-that-can-be-stockpiled-fast; Army Recognition: US Navy awards $257 million MK 48 MOD 7 torpedo contract (10 Sep 2026): https://www.armyrecognition.com/news/navy-news/2026/lockheed-martin-mk48-torpedo-257m-contract-us-navy.
- **usn_mk50_air** (weapon, Torpedo): historical 2,900,000 (published price, reference year 1992); current 1,190,000 (documented replacement estimate, source year 2014). Mk 50: average unit cost $2.9M (then-year, 1,000-round objective) as published by FAS; current is the Mk 54, its documented cheaper replacement (FY2014 $839,320, CPI to 2026). Current: the Mk 54's FY2014 839,320 x 334.4 / 236.7 (CPI-U 2014) = 1,185,757, rounded; a CPI conversion of the Mk 50 figure (6,912,046) is not used, because a documented replacement ranks first. Sources: FAS Military Analysis Network: MK-50 Advanced Lightweight Torpedo: https://man.fas.org/dod-101/sys/ship/weaps/mk-50.htm; StrategyPage: Air Weapons, November 7, 2004 (page read): https://www.strategypage.com/htmw/htairw/articles/20041107.aspx; Mark 54 lightweight torpedo - Wikipedia (infobox; Senate Report 113-44): https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_54_lightweight_torpedo; Federal Reserve Bank of Minneapolis: Consumer Price Index, 1913-: https://www.minneapolisfed.org/about-us/monetary-policy/inflation-calculator/consumer-price-index-1913-.
- **usn_mk54_1_ship** (weapon, Torpedo): historical 1,000,000 (model estimate, reference year 2004); current 1,190,000 (model estimate, source year 2014). Model estimate: Mk 54 Mod 1 priced as its own family's Mod 0 (same body and propulsion; sonar/processor update); no Mod 1 all-up-round price was found. Mk 54 Mod 0 figures (839,320 x 334.4 / 236.7 (CPI-U 2014) = 1,185,757, rounded); the FY2025 Mod 1 hardware kit alone is budgeted at about 1,152,000, so this is likely a floor. Sources: Mark 54 lightweight torpedo - Wikipedia: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_54_lightweight_torpedo; NavWeaps: Post-World War II Torpedoes of the United States (Mark 54 section): https://www.navweaps.com/Weapons/WTUS_PostWWII.htm; Mark 54 lightweight torpedo - Wikipedia (infobox; Senate Report 113-44): https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_54_lightweight_torpedo; Federal Reserve Bank of Minneapolis: Consumer Price Index, 1913-: https://www.minneapolisfed.org/about-us/monetary-policy/inflation-calculator/consumer-price-index-1913-.

### Researched, not shipped

377 items from the 23-24 October 2026 research. A refuted row failed the adversarial check; `left unknown` means the check agreed that no defensible figure exists yet. `Re-run` marks an item whose research stopped because the session's web search allowance ran out or the source host was blocked. Land items are kept for reference only: the tracker does not price land platforms.
